Formall<sub>y,</sub> a CFIMDP is defined as follo<sub>w</sub>s:

Definition 4.1 (Counterfactual Interval MDP (CFIMDP) [17]). Given an obser<sub>v</sub>ed <sub>p</sub>ath <sub>�</sub> and IMDP $M _ { \updownarrow } .$ <sub>,</sub> th<sub>e coun</sub>t<sub>er</sub>f<sub>ac</sub>t<sub>ua</sub>l <sub>pro</sub>b<sub>a</sub>bilit<sub>y</sub> f<sub>or</sub> <sub>eac</sub>h tr<sub>a</sub>n<sub>s</sub>iti<sub>o</sub>n in th<sub>e</sub> CFIMDP $\tilde { \mathcal { M } } _ { \tau } ^ { \updownarrow }$ i<sub>s</sub> d<sub>e</sub>fi<sub>ne</sub>d<sub>,</sub> f<sub>or</sub> $t = 0 , \ldots , | \tau | - 1$ as $\tilde { P } _ { t } ^ { \updownarrow } ( s ^ { \prime } \mid s , a ) = [ \tilde { P } _ { t } ^ { L B } ( s ^ { \prime } \mid s , a ) , \tilde { P } _ { t } ^ { U B } ( s ^ { \prime } \mid s , a ) ]$

Proposition 4.2 (Confidence propagation). Let $M _ { \updownarrow }$ be a datadriven IMDP, which contains the true MDP with probability at least 1 − �. Then the CFIMDP derived from $M _ { \updownarrow }$ contains the true CFMDP with probability at least 1 − � (proof in Appendix D).

## 4.3 Minimal Robust Counterfactual Interventions

Relation to actual causation. Minimal robust counterfactual interventions are closel<sub>y</sub> related to the notion of actual causes [11]. Informally, a set ofobserved actions is an actual cause ofan outcome if chan<sub>g</sub>in<sub>g</sub> those actions<sub>,</sub> while kee<sub>p</sub>in<sub>g</sub> all other actions at their <sub>o</sub>b<sub>serve</sub>d <sub>va</sub>l<sub>ues, wou</sub>ld h<sub>ave c</sub>h<sub>ange</sub>d th<sub>e coun</sub>t<sub>er</sub>f<sub>ac</sub>t<sub>ua</sub>l <sub>ou</sub>t<sub>come,</sub> and no strict subset of these actions has this <sub>p</sub>ro<sub>p</sub>ert<sub>y</sub>. ## 5 Evaluation

We evaluate our a<sub>pp</sub>roach alon<sub>g</sub> five axes: (1) its accurac<sub>y</sub> in of-<sub>p</sub>olic<sub>y</sub> evaluation tasks; (2) how its <sub>p</sub>olicies com<sub>p</sub>are a<sub>g</sub>ainst those <sub>o</sub>bt<sub>a</sub>i<sub>ne</sub>d f<sub>rom</sub> th<sub>e exac</sub>t CFIMDP $( { \mathrm { i . e . } }$ <sub>,</sub> th<sub>e</sub> CFIMDP d<sub>e</sub>ri<sub>ve</sub>d fr<sub>o</sub>m th<sub>e</sub> true MDP); (3) its robustness relative to the Gumbel-max a<sub>pp</sub>roach; (4) how well it identifies minimal action chan<sub>g</sub>es to achieve a desired counterfactual value; and (5) the trade-of of usin<sub>g</sub> conservative <sub>vs.</sub> ti<sub>g</sub>ht l<sub>ower</sub> b<sub>oun</sub>d<sub>s.</sub> W<sub>e</sub> <sub>eva</sub>l<sub>ua</sub>t<sub>e</sub> <sub>our</sub> <sub>approac</sub>h <sub>on</sub> <sub>a</sub> <sub>range</sub> <sub>o</sub>f MDP tasks, ran<sub>g</sub>in<sub>g</sub> from sim<sub>p</sub>le models (GridWorld and Frozen Lake) to more com<sub>p</sub>lex clinical and navi<sub>g</sub>ation tasks (Se<sub>p</sub>sis and Aircraft). # Robust Counterfactuals for Markov Decision Processes under Statistical and Causal Uncertainties (Supplementary Material)

## A Counterfactual Probability Bounds for MDPs

We co<sub>py</sub> the counterfactual <sub>p</sub>robabilit<sub>y</sub> bounds <sub>p</sub>roven b<sub>y</sub> Lall<sub>y</sub> et al. [17] below, under the assum<sub>p</sub>tions of counterfactual stabilit<sub>y</sub> and counterfactual monotonicit<sub>y</sub>:

Definition A.1 (Counterfactual stability). An MDP SCM (1) satisfies counterfactual stability if, given we observed the transition $s _ { t } , a _ { t } \to s _ { t + 1 }$ <sub>a</sub>t ti<sub>me</sub> �<sub>,</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>coun</sub>t<sub>er</sub>f<sub>ac</sub>t<sub>ua</sub>l <sub>ou</sub>t<sub>come</sub> <sub>un</sub>d<sub>er</sub> <sub>a</sub> dif<sub>eren</sub>t <sub>s</sub>t<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e-ac</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> <sub>pa</sub>i<sub>r</sub> $( s , a )$ <sub>w</sub>ill <sub>no</sub>t <sub>c</sub>h<sub>ange</sub> t<sub>o</sub> <sub>some</sub> $S _ { t + 1 } = s ^ { \prime } \neq s _ { t + 1 }$ <sub>un</sub>l<sub>ess</sub> $\frac { P ( s _ { t + 1 } \mid s , a ) } { P ( s _ { t + 1 } \mid s _ { t } , a _ { t } ) } <$ $\frac { P ( s ^ { \prime } \mid s , a ) } { P ( s ^ { \prime } \mid s _ { t } , a _ { t } ) } .$

Definition A.2 (Counterfactual monotonicity). An MDP SCM (1) satisfies counterfactual monotonicity if, upon observing the transition $s _ { t } , a _ { t } \to s _ { t + 1 } .$ th<sub>en,</sub> $\forall s \in S , a \in { \mathcal { A } } :$

(Mon1) $\tilde { P } _ { t } ( s _ { t + 1 } \mid s , a ) \geq P ( s _ { t + 1 } \mid s , a ) \mathrm { ( i . e . }$ ., observin<sub>g</sub> an outcome cannot make it less likel<sub>y</sub> in the counterfactual world), and

(Mon2) $\forall s ^ { \prime } \neq s _ { t + 1 }$ <sub>w</sub>ith $P ( s ^ { \prime } \mid s _ { t } , a _ { t } ) > 0 , \tilde { P } _ { t } ( s ^ { \prime } \mid s , a ) \le P ( s ^ { \prime } \mid s , a )$ (i.e., not observin<sub>g</sub> a <sub>p</sub>ossible outcome cannot make it more likel<sub>y</sub> in the counterfactual world).

and $G _ { q , t } ^ { \prime }$ is derived given $P _ { M } ( s ^ { \prime } \mid s _ { t } , a _ { t } ) = P _ { M _ { \uparrow } } ^ { U B } ( s ^ { \prime } \mid s _ { t } , a _ { t } )$ , and $\forall s ^ { \prime \prime } \neq s ^ { \prime } , P _ { M } ( s ^ { \prime \prime } \mid s _ { t } , a _ { t } ) = P _ { M _ { \uparrow } } ^ { L B } ( s ^ { \prime \prime } \mid s _ { t } , a _ { t } )$

## F Environment Descriptions and System Details

## F.1 GridWorld

The <sub>g</sub>rid also contains a hazardous<sub>,</sub> terminal state in the centre<sub>,</sub> and enterin<sub>g</sub> it incurs a <sub>p</sub>enalt<sub>y</sub> of −100. In our ex<sub>p</sub>eriments, we use two variants of the environment with diferent values of $p , p = 0 . 9 \ : ( \mathrm { i . e . }$ 90% chance of movin in the chosen direction) and $ p = 0 . 4$

## F.2 Frozen Lake

The Frozen Lake MDP is ada<sub>p</sub>ted from PRISM [10, 16]. In this environment, the a<sub>g</sub>ent navi<sub>g</sub>ates a 4×4 <sub>g</sub>rid to retrieve a frisbee, while avoidin<sub>g</sub> a set of holes scattered across the <sub>g</sub>rid. Since the ori<sub>g</sub>inal PRISM environment is formulated as a reachabilit<sub>y p</sub>roblem<sub>,</sub> the environment has been converted to a reward-based settin which ields a reward of 100 for successfull reachin the frisbee and a enalt of −100 for fallin i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>o a</sub> h<sub>o</sub>l<sub>e.</sub> B<sub>o</sub>th th<sub>e goa</sub>l <sub>s</sub>t<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e an</sub>d <sub>s</sub>t<sub>a</sub>t<sub>es w</sub>ith h<sub>o</sub>l<sub>es are</sub> t<sub>erm</sub>i<sub>na</sub>l<sub>.</sub>

## F.3 Sepsis

The Se<sub>p</sub>sis MDP [23] simulates <sub>p</sub>atient trajectories in a se<sub>p</sub>sis treatment settin<sub>g</sub>. At each time ste<sub>p</sub>, the <sub>p</sub>atient’s state is defined b<sub>y</sub> four vita si<sub>g</sub>ns (heart rate, blood <sub>p</sub>ressure, ox<sub>yg</sub>en concentration, and <sub>g</sub>lucose levels), each cate<sub>g</sub>orised as low, in normal ran<sub>g</sub>e, or hi<sub>g</sub>h. The RL a<sub>g</sub>ent controls three treatments (which can be switched on or of), with the <sub>g</sub>oal of dischar<sub>g</sub>in<sub>g</sub> the <sub>p</sub>atient (which occurs when at least three of the vital si<sub>g</sub>ns are in normal ran<sub>g</sub>e, and all three treatments are switched of). The <sub>p</sub>atient dies if at least three vital si<sub>g</sub>ns are out of normal ran<sub>g</sub>e at an<sub>y</sub> time ste<sub>p</sub>. Rewards are sha<sub>p</sub>ed b<sub>y</sub> the number of vital si<sub>g</sub>ns that are out of normal ran<sub>g</sub>e, ran<sub>g</sub>in<sub>g</sub> from −1000 for <sub>p</sub>atient death to +1000 for <sub>p</sub>atient dischar<sub>g</sub>e.

Since the ori<sub>g</sub>inal PRISM environment is form<sub>u</sub>lated as a reachabilit<sub>y p</sub>roblem<sub>,</sub> it has been converted to a reward-based settin<sub>g</sub>, which <sub>y</sub>ields a reward of +100 for reachin<sub>g</sub> the <sub>g</sub>oal or a <sub>p</sub>enalt<sub>y</sub> $\mathrm { o f } - 1 0 0$ f<sub>or co</sub>llidi<sub>ng w</sub>ith th<sub>e o</sub>th<sub>er</sub> aircraft.
